# Design review — the declared-schema direction (ADR-019) and the substrate that realizes it

You are a fresh-context reviewer, and this round is a **design review**,
not a code review. You have full repo access. Read the repo yourself; do
not trust any summary in this charter over the bytes.

Three earlier rounds already examined this code for accident and
sloppiness, and closed green. **They never examined the DIRECTION.** That
is this round's sole subject: is the design right, does it promise more
than it delivers, and what will it cost the people who have to build on
it next.

You REPORT. You fix nothing and you propose no edits to the repository.
Every finding you write is input to a pending decision by the project's
owner.

## 0. READ THIS FIRST — the time budget, and why it decides your output shape

**You have a HARD 20-minute wall-clock budget for this entire session. It
is enforced by an external process kill. A session that hits it produces
NO usable output — every finding you have not yet written out is
discarded.** An earlier session in this repo spent its whole budget
investigating, planned to write up at the end, and was killed mid-write-up.
All of its work was lost.

So the rule is: **write each finding out IN FULL, in the §4 format, the
moment you form it.** Do not accumulate findings for the end. Your closing
message should need to contain nothing but the counts and the §5
paragraph, because everything else is already written.

Budget shape: stop investigating at roughly the 12-minute mark and spend
the rest consolidating. Prefer **breadth at shallow depth**. If a check
looks like it will take more than about two minutes, write it down as
`unrun` with one sentence on what would settle it, and move on.

## 2. What the design is, in one page

The repo validates authored workflow-definition documents. Until this
work, the rules lived as hand-written code in two places — a walk over
the parsed YAML document, and an admission pass over the resulting value
— which disagreed about which rules ran on which of the two entry
channels (a file, or a value a caller constructs directly in code).

ADR-019 decided that structural rules become **declared schema**:

- a VOCABULARY of declaration attributes, expressed as types
  (`schema/vocabulary.ts`);
- a DECLARATION of the template format, expressed as data
  (`schema/templateFormat.ts`), each node carrying a `tag` and the
  ratified contract rows it realizes;
- ONE engine (`schema/engine.ts`) that applies the declaration to either
  channel;
- a separately-named NORMALIZER (`schema/normalizer.ts`) that computes
  the admitted form, because "a declaration says what is legal; it does
  not compute a value";
- a small remainder — the ADR calls it the RESIDUAL, seven named
  families — where prose and hand-written code still legislate.

The motivating measurement is in the ADR's Context: a prior prose-based
attempt produced repeated semantic restatement, and the second cleanup
sweep found as many fresh defects as the first. The ADR's bet is that
making rules DATA makes restatement structurally impossible.

The realizing build is complete: the hand-written walk is deleted, the
engine is live on both channels, and a parity exercise found zero
differences in finding paths and zero in finding messages.

## 3. The lenses

Work them in order and **emit findings as you go**.

**Lens 1 — the LOAD-BEARING CLAIMS. Stress them; do not restate them.**
For each claim below: is it true as stated, true only under conditions
the ADR does not name, or true of less than it sounds like? Say what a
COUNTEREXAMPLE would look like, concretely, and check whether one exists.

  a. *"One engine on both channels makes restatement structurally
     impossible."* Structurally impossible for WHAT, exactly? Count what
     the declaration actually covers versus what the surface's rules
     are. Is there anywhere a rule could still be stated twice?
  b. *"The declaration is data, not code."* Test it against the bytes.
     What in `templateFormat.ts` behaves as code — is interpreted, is
     compiled, or carries control flow — even though it is spelled as
     data? Does anything in the file survive `JSON.stringify` unchanged,
     and does it matter either way? The ADR calls it "a frozen
     declaration object"; check what is actually frozen.
  c. *"The residual is exactly seven families."* Is every hand-written
     rule that remains a member of one of the seven, honestly? Look
     especially at anything the build placed in a family whose stated
     definition does not quite fit it. A remainder that is really an
     eighth kind, filed under a seventh name, is a finding.
  d. *"Channel symmetry becomes structural rather than argued."* The
     declaration marks some attributes as belonging to one channel.
     Count how many such marks exist and what decides them. Is symmetry
     now structural, or is the asymmetry simply written down in a new
     place?

**Lens 2 — FUTURE-CONSUMER STRESS.** Three consumers are already
scheduled. For each, walk what they will actually have to do against the
current design and name what will hurt, mislead, or need rework:

  a. **A contract author (next phase)** will write a ratified prose
     document whose rows CITE declaration tags instead of restating
     attributes. Are the tags stable, addressable and granular enough to
     cite? Is there a tag for everything a row would want to point at,
     and is there anything a row must point at that has no tag?
  b. **A packet author (the phase after)** will project an
     implementation task onto these declarations. What must they read to
     know what the declaration already guarantees? Is that answerable
     from the declaration alone?
  c. **A later migration act** may bring three currently-excluded
     validators onto the engine. The build recorded why it could not:
     a module-boundary rule (`v3/eslint.config.mjs`) and the shape of an
     injected registration's interface (`v3/src/ports/gate.ts`). Read
     both. Is the recorded reason accurate, and what would that
     migration have to change?

**Lens 3 — THE LOCK GAP.** The ADR's D4 acknowledges that a code file
can be edited without the ratification act that governs ratified prose,
and says the fix is to extend the act to the schema file's bytes — which
happens at the NEXT phase, with the contract. So: **today, what stops
`templateFormat.ts` from being edited?** Search the repo for any check,
lint rule, test, tool or convention that names that file or guards its
content. Report what you find and what you do not. Then answer directly:
is the gap between now and the next phase acceptable, or is it a finding?

**Lens 4 — VOCABULARY COHERENCE.** Read `vocabulary.ts` as a language
specification, which is what it is.

  a. Is it self-consistent? Does every documented attribute mean the same
     thing everywhere it appears?
  b. Can a construct be honestly MISREAD — a declaration author writes
     something reasonable that means something else, or means nothing?
     Name the specific construct and the specific misreading.
  c. The build argued that a field typed to ONE literal value needs no
     runtime comparison because the type system enforces it. Is that
     argument sound? Where does it hold and where does it not?

**Lens 5 — THE MAINTENANCE PATH.** Take one concrete rule of the
template format — your choice, but say which — and walk the ACTUAL path
a future maintainer follows to change it: which files they open, in what
order, what tells them they got it right, and what would still pass if
they got it wrong. **Where can they be wrong SILENTLY?** A change that
compiles, passes the suite, and quietly stops enforcing something is the
outcome to hunt here.

## 3.1 What the closed rounds already covered — do not re-hunt these

Three rounds already swept for: declaration attributes with no reader;
stale or unstamped numbers in the write-ups; finding-message and
finding-path drift against the deleted implementation; whether the
engine contains a rule-specific branch; whether test fixtures
discriminate. Their record is in
`v3/implementation/ch13-rederivation-arm/p3-build/` (three charters,
three verdicts, and `phase-close-report.md`). **Read the phase-close
report first** so you spend this round's budget on new ground.

If you happen to trip over an instance of a closed class, one line is
enough — say it is a closed class and move on.

## 4. Evidence bar and finding format

This is a design review, so **a reasoned finding is legal** where a
command cannot settle the question — but the SCENARIO must be specific:
a named construct, a named file, a named future act, and what concretely
goes wrong. "This might be confusing" is not a finding. "An author who
writes X, following the doc at line N, gets Y and no error" is.

Where a claim IS runnable, run it. Experimental work goes under `/tmp`.

Classify every finding as exactly one of:

- **design-error** — the design is wrong or will not do what it is for;
- **over-promise** — the design works but a stated claim is wider than
  what it delivers;
- **future-trap** — it works now and will bite a named future act;
- **confirmed-sound** — you stress-tested a claim and it held. **Report
  these too.** A round that reports only problems has not told the reader
  which parts they can rely on.

```
### F<n> — <one-line title>
CLASS: design-error | over-promise | future-trap | confirmed-sound
LENS:  1a..1d | 2a..2c | 3 | 4a..4c | 5
WHAT:  the finding in one sentence
WHERE: file:line (or the ADR section)
EVIDENCE:
  <exact command and its real output, OR the specific scenario:
   who does what, following which line, and what they get>
WHY IT MATTERS: the consequence, concretely
```

Do not include a fix sketch. Fixes are not this round's output.

## 5. The closing message — and the question you must answer

Only this, because everything else is already written:

- your tree's HEAD;
- counts by class: design-error / over-promise / future-trap /
  confirmed-sound;
- which lenses you completed, partially ran, or did not reach;
- and then, **the question the owner is actually asking**:

  > **Is the approach sound?**

  Answer it in ONE paragraph, hedge-free. Not "it depends" and not a
  summary of your findings — a judgement. If the answer is yes with
  conditions, name the conditions. If the answer is no, say what the
  design would have to become. You have read the ADR, its evidence base
  and the code that realizes it; you are better placed to answer this
  than anyone who has been living inside the work.

## 6. Rules of engagement

- **Do not modify the repository.** Not one byte, tracked or untracked.
  A byte guard runs before and after this session and any change
  invalidates the entire verdict. Work under `/tmp`.
- **Do not propose new machinery**, no new modules, attributes or tools.
- **Proportionality.** If the smallest honest correction to something is
  larger than the thing itself, that observation is the finding.
- **Do not manufacture findings to fill the round, and do not soften a
  real one to be agreeable.** A design that survives is a result worth
  reporting; so is one that does not.

# P3 build — phase-close report

The second arc of phase P3 (the ch13 re-derivation plan §3): the
declared-schema substrate authorized by ADR-019, built, parity-gated,
switched, tested and arm-verified. This report is the STOP 2 artifact.

## Trajectory (every number derived at write time)

- **Build day 2** — the arc's first commit is `3466d241`, 2026-08-05;
  this report is written 2026-08-06.
- **Arm rounds 3** — `ls ch13-rederivation-arm/p3-build/arm-*-out.txt`
  returns 3. Findings by round: **6 · 6 · 0**, all in scope, all folded,
  zero carried.
- **Size**, at `HEAD`: substrate 2396 raw / 1743 code-only
  (vocabulary 384 · engine 1183 · declaration 545 · normalizer 140 ·
  surface 144), plus `admit.ts` 68 and `load.ts` 268. The hand-written
  code it replaced was 1366 raw / 965 code-only at `7b6bfbba`, the commit
  before the build. **Ratio 1.75× raw, 1.81× code-only.**
- **Suite 1919 / 1919**, 72 files; `v3:typecheck` and `v3:lint` clean.

## What was built

`v3/src/definition/schema/` — five modules with one job each:

| Module | What it is |
|---|---|
| `vocabulary.ts` | ADR-019 §2.2's admitted constructs, as TYPES. No logic. |
| `templateFormat.ts` | D4's canonical home: the template surface's declaration, frozen DATA, every node carrying its `tag` and the ratified rows it realizes. |
| `engine.ts` | The validator over declaration data — one computation for both channels. |
| `normalizer.ts` | D3's second, separately-named capability: derivation. |
| `templateSurface.ts` | The composition, plus the audited residual, wired into one finding stream. |

`definition/validate.ts` (898 lines) is deleted. `definition/admit.ts`
went from 468 lines of rules to 68 lines of two entry points and the A6
brand mint. `definition/load.ts` calls the surface once.

## The parity gate (ADR-019 D5) — the load-bearing result

Full record: `v3/implementation/p3-parity-gate.md`.

**Both PRE-NAMED delta classes came back EMPTY.** Across 105 divergence
records over 1830 executed cases: for every finding path both
implementations emit, `{path, message, code}` is identical. Zero path
deltas. Zero message deltas. The audit's §5 F3 called path grain "the
parity gate's highest-yield target"; the `at:` / `keyLaneAt:` /
`laneOrder:` attributes it recommended are why it yielded nothing.

The deltas that did exist were ratified before the switch:

- **Class A, channel symmetry** — structural rules now run on the
  direct-construction channel too. This is D1's own text realized, and
  the mechanism the audit's F2 predicted would dissolve ch13-C7's entry
  belt. It cost four repaired fixtures, three of which wanted an
  admitted-but-inconsistent template and now drift one deliberately
  instead of relying on admission's blindness.
- **Class B, lane order** — same finding set, walk and admission
  interleaved. No case affected.
- **Class C, the `(b′)` collision — WITHDRAWN.** I reported that an
  empty `steps` map could not both fire the round-membership lane and
  suppress the `start` lane once the split dissolved, and the ruling
  authorized amending the disposition. Re-reading ch11-P4's F7 showed the
  premise was mine, not the direction's: ch8-C21's suppression binds
  MISSING or WRONG-KIND containers only, and an empty map is a valid map
  of its kind. Removing one wrongly-declared `gating` attribute restored
  `(b′)` exactly. No amendment was made.

**One measurement refinement, recorded because it changes what a future
gate should replay:** D5's 362-case corpus reproduces exactly, but it
UNDER-MEASURES this surface — `admitTemplate` is the direct channel's
entry and is called from the testkit, kernel, lifecycle and trace suites,
not only the seven named files. Two thirds of the affected cases live
outside the corpus.

## The arm frame

Its own three-round frame; the audit arc's rounds did not carry over.

**Round 1 — 6 in-scope, 0 carried, 1 unrun.** Verdict: the central claim
does NOT survive. It was right. The declaration carried a whole SUBSTRATE
block — strict UTF-8, the YAML version, duplicate keys, warning
promotion, alias resolution, the stage list, the path grammar — and the
runtime read exactly one field of it. Everything else was decoration
wearing an authority's costume: the precise false-green this direction
exists to end, committed by the build that ends it. Folded by making the
substrate carry only what is CONSUMED (`load.ts` now reads the decode
wording, the directive version and message, the duplicate-key wording and
the internal-failure belt) or ASSERTED (`codes`, now checked against
every issue code the declaration assigns). Four further attributes with
no reader were deleted; `valueRaw` was implemented, used five times, and
missing from the vocabulary's own "closed placeholder set".

**Round 2 — 6 in-scope, 0 carried, 0 unrun.** Corrections 2–5 landed;
1 and 6 did not. One real contradiction (`nonempty.gating`'s doc stated
the opposite of what the `steps` declaration does after the `(b′)`
correction), three more declared-but-unread fields, and two defects of my
own in prose: numbers stamped "at HEAD" and a replay recipe ending in a
placeholder pointing at a tool that was never shipped.

**Round 3 — 0 / 0 / 0.** All six landed. The claim was tested rather than
re-read: a template value and its YAML equivalent, admitted and loaded,
producing the same admitted result and the same path and message for a
planted defect; the declared source-form asymmetry behaving as declared;
both residual rules firing exactly once.

**The round shape, stated plainly: 6 · 6 · 0.** It did not fall
monotonically. Round 2 matching round 1 is the honest number, and the
class shifted — round 1 found one whole decorative block, round 2 found
three prose defects and three single-valued fields.

## What stayed prose or code, and why

- **R3** the existential cross-rule over resolved registrations
  (ch11-C19 → ch12-C5): a conditional over a DERIVED property of an
  injected object; a `when:` general enough to express it is an
  expression language.
- **R7** the uses-scoped source ladder on two gate-config integers
  (ch11-C12's source half) — **R7's first live member**. Its declaration
  exists (`[vc-authored-int]`), but realizing it in the engine needs the
  `delegate` hand-off to carry the CHANNEL into the registration, which
  changes the ratified `GateRegistration` port shape. Refusing a
  construct that would serve one row is D7's test applied honestly, and
  R7 was kept open at the ratification for exactly this.
- **The three delegated gate-config schemas** stay as built. Not a
  judgement call: ch11-P2a G1 is lint-enforced and permits `gates/` to
  value-import `domain/` and `ports/` only. Under D2 and D6 that is a
  separate per-surface act. This is a reduction against the kickoff's
  letter and was ruled on at STOP 1.

## The ADR's own tripwires, checked

- **D9.1** (a single-use construct admitted to make one row fit) — did
  not fire. The one candidate (a source lane scoped by a sibling's value)
  was REFUSED, and its row went to R7.
- **D9.2** (the engine acquires a per-rule branch) — did not fire. Where
  measured lanes disagreed on a grain, the disagreement was DECLARED
  (`at:`, `laneOrder:`, `gating:`, `channel:`) rather than branched.
  Round 1's Lens B hunted this specifically and found none.
- **D9.3** (the residual grows past the seven audited families) — did not
  fire. R7 gained its first member; no family was created.
- **Plan §6 proportionality** — **FIRED**, at 1.85× when first measured.
  Now 1.75× / 1.81× after two folds deleted data that was doing nothing.
  Ruled at STOP 1: re-measure at the P4/P5 boundary, where the engine's
  fixed cost either amortizes over a second surface or does not.

## Open, carried forward

1. **The proportionality re-measurement at the P4/P5 boundary** (the
   standing ruling).
2. **The declaration-as-data-through-the-port boundary candidate**
   (`9790b800`) — the route by which the gate-config schemas could join.
3. **A criterion question for the next guard-machinery loop.** Round 2
   reported fields whose single literal value no machine compares. Round
   3 was given a refinement — report such a field only if a DIFFERENT
   legal value would change nothing observable — and rejected zero
   candidates by it, because it found none. The refinement is therefore
   untested and is offered as a candidate rule, not a ratified one.

     1	# ADR-019: structural definition rules become declared schema, validated by one engine on both channels
     2	
     3	Status: accepted
     4	Date: 2026-08-05
     5	Links: supersedes — · amends — · depends-on ADR-011 · related ADR-003, ADR-012, ADR-013
     6	
     7	## Context
     8	
     9	The ch13 prose line ended at a MEASURED semantic drift floor: 28
    10	registered rules · 6 literal restatements caught by the pointer-lint ·
    11	9 semantic paraphrases in the first full arm sweep · 8 FRESH ones in
    12	the second sweep after the nine were folded. The second number did not
    13	shrink; it came from new places. The measured cause was the worklist:
    14	the conversion's index was a hand-built mirror table, and the
    15	paraphrases lived exactly where that table never looked (five rules had
    16	zero pointers; four of the table's first nine rows were wrong or
    17	incomplete). The general form — **a lint confines LITERALS, a mirror
    18	list names MEANINGS, and prose is not indexable by meaning.** For
    19	restatement to become structurally impossible, the rules must be DATA.
    20	
    21	The user raised the structural form of this at the ch13 boundary
    22	(process-log 2026-08-02, commit `4c1d481f`): every new format surface
    23	(ch8, ch11, ch12, ch13) re-legislates the same failure space in
    24	ratified prose — container preconditions, key/value lanes, unknown
    25	keys, null/scalar/array forgeries, suppression edges, path grammar,
    26	channel symmetry. The direction was ratified 2026-08-03 and this ADR is
    27	its P3 realization.
    28	
    29	**The basis is an enumeration, not an impression.**
    30	`v3/implementation/schema-expressiveness-audit.md` — the bytes this ADR
    31	was RATIFIED against carried sha256
    32	`f779855f0a0823020c71951873a3a21244500ecd6644179cb78ac1f3045d8da7`
    33	(classification 59/5/6); the ratification act's own D8 ruling then
    34	moved two rows and resolved R7 empty, so the standing document is
    35	sha256
    36	`bfed821037786fa151f9a9c0f3b25d1142917056f10d52bb1dda5ddb4da6e57d`
    37	(classification 61/3/6). Both pins are recorded because the act changed
    38	its own basis, and only in the direction the act itself ruled — the
    39	audit could not have moved itself.
    40	
    41	The audit classifies every ratified rule of the four format surfaces
    42	against a proposed declaration form, and was verified by two bounded
    43	fresh-context arm rounds (10 findings then 5, all folded;
    44	`ch13-rederivation-arm/p3/`). Its measured result, at the ratified
    45	basis:
    46	
    47	- **125 contract rows** (ch8 38 · ch11 41 · ch12 27 · ch13 19). Of the
    48	  **70** that carry a definition-validation obligation: **59 fully
    49	  declarable · 5 mixed · 6 residual** (**61 · 3 · 6** after D8's
    50	  ruling). The other 55 legislate runtime,
    51	  CLI, store, port or process and are marked non-lane with a per-row
    52	  reason. Zero unclassified rows.
    53	- **112 finding-emit sites** in today's validator and the three
    54	  delegated gate-config schemas — every one mapped to its owning row.
    55	- The residual is **seven named families**, not a vague remainder (D8's
    56	  ruling then resolved one of them empty, leaving six with members).
    57	- The plan's predicted residual was CHECKED: event-grain suppression
    58	  and per-occurrence duplicates REFUTED (declarable); reference
    59	  resolution and unreferenced hygiene PARTIALLY refuted (the
    60	  intra-document forms are declarable with the selector vocabulary
    61	  ch8-C16 already ships). Five families the prediction did not name
    62	  were found, one of which forces D3 below.
    63	
    64	## Decision
    65	
    66	### D1 — the direction
    67	
    68	**Structural rules become per-surface DECLARED SCHEMA — key classes,
    69	containers, grammars, defaults and normalization as DATA — consumed by
    70	ONE engine that validates BOTH the file-walk and direct-construction
    71	channels. Ratified prose remains ONLY for the audited semantic
    72	residual.**
    73	
    74	Channel symmetry becomes structural rather than argued: attributes that
    75	read source text (`sourceForm`, key-stringness, the substrate block)
    76	are declared once and marked file-scoped; the engine runs them where a
    77	source exists and skips them where none does. This retires the
    78	hand-partitioned "realization split" that ch11-C40 and ch13-C19 each
    79	spent ratified prose defending.
    80	
    81	The residual — the ONLY territory where prose keeps legislating
    82	behaviour — is exactly the audit's seven families: **R1** value-shaped
    83	reference resolution · **R2** unreferenced hygiene with a template-wide
    84	skip · **R3** existential cross-rules over resolved registrations ·
    85	**R4** admitted-form derivation · **R5** cross-artifact checks · **R6**
    86	substrate-owned wording (a parity residual, no obligation) · **R7** rows
    87	whose only declaration uses a construct this ADR has not admitted (D8).
    88	
    89	### D2 — scope
    90	
    91	**The TEMPLATE surface, now. Nothing else is built.** The other
    92	validated surfaces in the repo are NAMED EXTENSION POINTS and each
    93	needs its own ratified act before any declaration is written for it:
    94	the `GateDecision` stdout contract (ch11-C25), the `GateInvocation`
    95	stdin wire (ch11-C23), the CLI `--run-overrides` input (ch12-C9/C20),
    96	the operator-intent wire (ch12-C13), and the EC emit-contract surface
    97	(plan §1.3's fifth candidate — deliberately excluded from the audit as
    98	having no ratified grammar).
    99	
   100	### D3 — the engine owes a SECOND capability, named
   101	
   102	The audit's R4 is not a leftover lane: admission both VALIDATES and
   103	PRODUCES the admitted form (`advancesRound` expanded per transition,
   104	the effective gate config materialized, ch13-C17's rebuild). **A
   105	declaration says what is legal; it does not compute a value.** The
   106	build therefore carries a NORMALIZER beside the validator, driven by
   107	declared hooks, and this ADR names it so that "schema" is never read as
   108	covering it. Plain `default:` materialization IS declarable and stays
   109	in the schema; only derivation is the normalizer's.
   110	
   111	### D4 — the canonical home: ONE authority, pointers everywhere else
   112	
   113	**RECOMMENDED: the declaration lives in the CODE tree as a data-only
   114	module under the definition module (ADR-011) — `v3/src/definition/
   115	schema/templateFormat.ts`, a frozen declaration object with no
   116	executable logic — and the contract carries DECISIONS plus declaration
   117	POINTERS, restating no attribute.** The ratification act names the
   118	schema file's bytes exactly as the contract-draft equality lock names
   119	the C-rows, so the lock is a MECHANISM, not a location.
   120	
   121	The weighing, recorded because it is the load-bearing choice here:
   122	
   123	- *Ratification-lock pull* — put the declaration in the contract
   124	  markdown. It inherits the ratified-bytes lock for free. Rejected as
   125	  the home: the engine would then parse ratified prose to get its
   126	  rules, which is the exact indexing-prose-by-meaning failure this
   127	  whole line exists to end.
   128	- *Code-consumption pull* — put it in the code tree. The engine
   129	  imports data; a diff is reviewable; the declaration is type-checked.
   130	  Its weakness is that a code file can be edited without a
   131	  ratification act — and that weakness is closed by extending the act
   132	  to the file's bytes, which costs one hash line per act.
   133	- **Two authorities is the outcome neither pull may produce.** The
   134	  contract states no structural attribute; it states decisions and
   135	  cites tags. A rule visible in both places is a defect, and the
   136	  cheapest tripwire is the tag-closure check the audit already ran
   137	  twice by hand (every tag defined is cited, every tag cited is
   138	  defined).
   139	
   140	### D5 — the parity gate
   141	
   142	**Before the engine replaces any implemented lane, the EXISTING
   143	fixture corpus is replayed against it and must produce identical
   144	verdicts, identical finding PATHS and identical MESSAGES — or an
   145	approved delta list, ratified before the switch, never after.**
   146	
   147	The corpus, measured 2026-08-05 by `grep -cE '^\s*(it|test)\('`: **362
   148	cases** — `validate.test.ts` 124 · `load.test.ts` 76 · `admit.test.ts`
   149	59 · `process.test.ts` 59 · `fileDefinitionStore.test.ts` 19 ·
   150	`threshold.test.ts` 13 · `previousReviewerVerdict.test.ts` 12.
   151	
   152	This is a DERIVED claim about behaviour that does not exist yet; its
   153	NAMED MEASURER is the build's parity gate itself. Two delta classes are
   154	pre-named by the audit so they arrive as decisions rather than
   155	surprises: **path grain** (duplicate terminal ids report at `terminal`,
   156	not `terminal[i]`; every key-class lane reports at its containing map —
   157	an engine written naively silently moves these addresses) and
   158	**message wording** (nine measured messages embed rule-specific
   159	rationale; either the declaration carries a literal `message:` or the
   160	delta list authorizes the engine's own wording).
   161	
   162	### D6 — retrofit policy
   163	
   164	**Existing surfaces stay as built.** Migrating a surface onto the
   165	engine is a deliberate per-surface BOUNDARY act with its own parity
   166	gate and its own ratification — never housekeeping, never folded into a
   167	packet that is doing something else, never a "while we are here". A
   168	surface that is never migrated is a legitimate end state.
   169	
   170	### D7 — the format-growth rule
   171	
   172	New expressiveness is a RATIFIED AMENDMENT, never silent, in two
   173	flavours that must not be confused:
   174	
   175	- **A new NODE declaration** (a key, a grammar, a default) is ordinary
   176	  additive format growth under plan §8.2 — the realizing chapter's
   177	  contract row plus the schema edit, in one act.
   178	- **A new VOCABULARY CONSTRUCT** changes what the engine can express at
   179	  all, and therefore amends THIS ADR. It is admitted only under the
   180	  audit's **≥2 independent ratified rows** test; a construct serving
   181	  exactly one row is refused and that row keeps a prose lane instead.
   182	
   183	### D8 — the two open constructs — RULED: BOTH ACCEPTED (user, 2026-08-05)
   184	
   185	Both were single-use, and the audit deliberately refused to bank their
   186	coverage until this act ruled. **Both are ACCEPTED**, on the ground
   187	that neither is a NEW construct:
   188	
   189	- `memberOf: keys(@catalog)` — a selector root that is an INJECTED set
   190	  rather than a document node (ch11-C8's `uses` resolution): admitted
   191	  as a GENERALIZATION of the existing selector root. A set is a set;
   192	  the relation, the finding form and the code carrier are unchanged.
   193	- per-member `code:` inside an enum (ch11-C16's `failInstance` earning
   194	  a distinct issue code): admitted as the existing `code` attribute
   195	  applied at MEMBER grain, not as a new attribute.
   196	
   197	**The D9 tripwire was examined at this ruling, not skirted** — recorded
   198	so a later reader can check the reasoning rather than trust it.
   199	Tripwire 1 fires on admitting a single-use CONSTRUCT to make one row
   200	fit. Neither ruling adds a construct: both widen the domain of a
   201	construct that already passes the ≥2-row test (selectors, 7 rows;
   202	`code:`, 7 rows). Had either required a genuinely new construct for its
   203	single row, the correct outcome would have been the prose lane.
   204	
   205	Consequence, executed in the audit at this act: residual **R7 is
   206	RESOLVED EMPTY** (kept as a named family with its record, so a future
   207	single-use candidate has a home and the history stays legible), and
   208	ch11-C8 and ch11-C16 move from `H` to `S`. The standing classification
   209	is therefore **61 fully declarable · 3 mixed · 6 residual** of the 70
   210	obligation-bearing rows.
   211	
   212	### D9 — the falsifiability criterion
   213	
   214	**The direction is declared FAILED, and this ADR deprecated, if
   215	structural rules cannot be expressed without per-rule special cases.**
   216	The smell is code wearing a declaration's costume. Three concrete
   217	tripwires, any one of which forces a scope review rather than a
   218	work-around:
   219	
   220	1. a single-use construct is admitted into the vocabulary to make one
   221	   row fit (D7's test bypassed);
   222	2. the engine acquires a per-rule branch — a literal `if (rule === X)`
   223	   — to reproduce a measured verdict, path or message;
   224	3. the declared residual grows past the seven audited family ids —
   225	   six carrying members, R7 resolved empty at D8 — during contract-v2
   226	   authoring, without a ratified amendment saying so.
   227	
   228	## Alternatives Considered
   229	
   230	- **Keep ratified prose plus a hand-written validator (the status
   231	  quo).** Rejected on measurement, not taste: the drift floor above is
   232	  what this shape produces, and its second sweep did not shrink.
   233	- **Adopt an off-the-shelf schema language (JSON Schema, Zod, and
   234	  friends) as the ratified artifact.** Rejected AS THE ARTIFACT: none
   235	  of them expresses the things the parity gate is made of — the
   236	  finding-path GRAIN (`at: index|container`), the container-precondition
   237	  suppression semantics, the source-form ladder over YAML nodes
   238	  (anchor/tag/alias-free raw text), the issue-`code` carrier, or the
   239	  channel-scoped attributes. Zod additionally is code, which forfeits
   240	  the declaration-as-data property that the whole line rests on. NOT
   241	  rejected as an implementation detail: generating onto an existing
   242	  validator core stays open at build time, provided the ratified
   243	  artifact remains our declaration.
   244	- **Put the declaration in the contract markdown (D4's rejected pull).**
   245	  Rejected as the home; kept as the pointer side.
   246	- **Build the engine first and declare afterwards.** Rejected: that is
   247	  precisely how today's validator grew, and the audit is the artifact
   248	  that had to be written to find out what it actually does.
   249	- **Migrate every surface at once.** Rejected by D6 and by the P1
   250	  overbuild lesson (process-log 2026-08-04): a guard that grows faster
   251	  than what it guards is the failure mode this phase already paid for.
   252	
   253	## IC-N Screen (mandatory)
   254	
   255	No. This decision changes how definition-time structural rules are
   256	EXPRESSED and validated; it touches no banned kernel shape — no
   257	deterministic replay of actor/LLM work, no leader-per-shard
   258	coordination, no event-sourcing as the source of truth, no
   259	reconciler/outbox for kernel state. This screen does not bypass the
   260	model↔code divergence stop: the audit's classification changes no
   261	model meaning, and any row whose MEANING would move travels the
   262	contract-reopen path in its own act.
   263	
   264	## Consequences
   265	
   266	- **Positive.** Restatement becomes structurally impossible for the 59
   267	  declarable rows — there is one place a structural rule can live.
   268	  Channel asymmetry stops being an argued property (D1). The residual
   269	  is a NAMED list of seven families instead of an unknown remainder, so
   270	  a future reader can see exactly where prose still legislates. The
   271	  ch13-C7 entry belt and its normalization class account for THREE of
   272	  that draft's four ratified reopens (the 2026-08-01 direct-channel act
   273	  that reopened C7 itself, and the 2026-08-02 pair on C17/C8(c)) — and
   274	  the belt is DERIVED to dissolve under one engine on both channels,
   275	  its measurer being the P4 panel's channel-symmetry family plus the
   276	  parity gate.
   277	- **Negative.** A second machine exists (D3's normalizer) and must be
   278	  kept honest, or "schema" will be read as covering derivation. The
   279	  contract becomes less self-contained for a human ratifier: decisions
   280	  live in prose, attributes in the schema file, and the ratification
   281	  act must name both byte sets. The parity gate is real work whose cost
   282	  lands on whoever migrates a surface. None of the benefit is realized
   283	  at this ADR — it is realized at P4/P5.
   284	- **Neutral.** Today's validator keeps working unchanged under D6; this
   285	  ADR authorizes a direction and a scope, not a rewrite. The issue-code
   286	  namespace, the finding forms and the CLI machine shape are unchanged
   287	  by design — they are inputs to the parity gate, not outputs of it.
   288	
   289	## Verification
   290	
   291	- **The parity gate (D5)** is the primary proof: 362 existing cases
   292	  replayed, verdict/path/message identical or an approved delta list.
   293	- **The tag-closure check (D4)**: every declaration tag cited by a
   294	  contract row exists in the schema file and vice versa — run twice by
   295	  hand during the audit, both difference lists empty at 51/51.
   296	- **The ≥2-row admission test (D7)** is recorded per construct in the
   297	  audit's §2.2, each cell enumerating its rows; a construct that cannot
   298	  produce its second row is refused.
   299	- **The falsifiability tripwires (D9)** are checked at the P4 contract
   300	  authoring and at every migration act; firing one opens a scope
   301	  review, not a work-around.
   302	- The audit itself is the standing basis document, pinned by hash in
   303	  Context.
   304	
   305	## Related
   306	
   307	`v3/implementation/schema-expressiveness-audit.md` (the enumeration
   308	this ADR rests on) · `v3/implementation/ch13-rederivation-plan.md`
   309	(P3's charter; the phase order P1 → P3 → P4 → P5) · the four format
   310	contracts `ch8-template-format`, `ch11-gate-format`,
   311	`ch12-runtime-core`, `ch13-context-block` (superseded — its rows remain
   312	the ratified decision record) · ADR-011 (the definition module, D4's
   313	home) · ADR-012 (the yaml dependency behind the substrate block) ·
   314	ADR-013 (the injected gate registry D8's first construct resolves
   315	against) · process-log 2026-08-02 (the boundary candidate `4c1d481f`),
   316	2026-08-03 (the ratified direction), 2026-08-04 (the overbuild reset
   317	whose four rules bound this phase), 2026-08-05 (P1's close, P2 struck).
     1	/**
     2	 * ADR-019 (accepted 2026-08-05): the declaration VOCABULARY, as types.
     3	 *
     4	 * This module is TYPES ONLY — it defines the shape a surface declaration
     5	 * may take, and nothing else. The declaration itself is data
     6	 * (`templateFormat.ts`, D4's canonical home); the machines that consume
     7	 * it are `engine.ts` (the validator) and `normalizer.ts` (D3's second,
     8	 * separately-named capability).
     9	 *
    10	 * Every construct below is one of ADR-019 §2.2's admitted vocabulary
    11	 * entries, each of which passed the audit's ≥2-independent-ratified-rows
    12	 * test (`v3/implementation/schema-expressiveness-audit.md` §2.2, standing
    13	 * sha256 `bfed8210…`). Adding a construct is a D7 amendment of the ADR,
    14	 * never a silent edit here.
    15	 */
    16	
    17	/**
    18	 * A message template: literal text with `{placeholder}` slots the engine
    19	 * interpolates (vocabulary #18 — `message:`, the PARITY carrier; ADR-019
    20	 * D5 pre-names message wording as a delta class, and a literal template
    21	 * is the choice that keeps the measured wording byte-identical).
    22	 *
    23	 * The closed placeholder set, all supplied by the engine from the node's
    24	 * own evaluation context — never rule-specific:
    25	 *
    26	 * - `{path}`      the finding's own path
    27	 * - `{key}`       the offending key, raw
    28	 * - `{keyJson}`   the offending key, JSON-quoted
    29	 * - `{value}`     the offending value, DESCRIBED (`a map`/`a list`/literal)
    30	 * - `{valueRaw}`  the offending value, rendered bare (no quoting) — the
    31	 *                 form the measured messages that embed a value inside
    32	 *                 their own quotes need
    33	 * - `{valueJson}` the offending value, JSON-quoted
    34	 * - `{source}`    the offending value's raw source slice, JSON-quoted
    35	 * - `{grammar}`   the grammar's regular-expression source
    36	 * - `{members}`   the enum/allowlist members, comma-joined
    37	 * - `{keys}`      the node's legal keyset, comma-joined
    38	 * - `{label}`     the referencing site's label for a value class
    39	 * - `{ownerKey}`  the key of the nearest enclosing OPEN-map entry
    40	 */
    41	export type MessageTemplate = string;
    42	
    43	/**
    44	 * A path into the value graph, in the declaration's own address form:
    45	 * `$` root, dotted segments, `*` for "every entry of an open map", and a
    46	 * leading `..` for one level up from the citing node.
    47	 */
    48	export type DeclPath = string;
    49	
    50	/** vocabulary #12 — the selector roots. `injected` is D8's ADMITTED
    51	 * widening: a selector root that is an injected SET rather than a
    52	 * document node (a set is a set; the relation and finding form are
    53	 * unchanged). */
    54	export type Selector =
    55	  | { readonly keysOf: DeclPath }
    56	  | { readonly valuesOf: DeclPath }
    57	  | { readonly collect: DeclPath }
    58	  | { readonly union: readonly Selector[] }
    59	  | { readonly injected: "gateCatalog" };
    60	
    61	/**
    62	 * vocabulary #12 — a membership relation between the citing node's value
    63	 * (or its keys) and a selector's set.
    64	 *
    65	 * `at` is the finding's PATH GRAIN and exists because the measured lanes
    66	 * DISAGREE (audit §5 F3): the catalog-resolution lane reports at the
    67	 * BINDING, not at `…uses`. `code` is vocabulary #17.
    68	 */
    69	export interface MembershipRule {
    70	  readonly relation: "memberOf" | "keysSubsetOf" | "disjointFrom";
    71	  readonly target: Selector;
    72	  /** `self` = the citing node's own path (the default); `container` = its
    73	   * parent's path. */
    74	  readonly at?: "self" | "container";
    75	  readonly code?: string;
    76	  readonly message: MessageTemplate;
    77	  /** vocabulary #13 — suppression beyond the implicit container rule. */
    78	  readonly dependsOn?: readonly string[];
    79	}
    80	
    81	/** vocabulary #11 — the duplicate lane and its path grain. */
    82	export interface UniqueRule {
    83	  readonly grain: "perOccurrence";
    84	  readonly at: "index" | "container";
    85	  readonly message: MessageTemplate;
    86	}
    87	
    88	/**
    89	 * The keyset lane ORDER. It is declared, not hard-coded, for the same
    90	 * reason `at:` is (audit §5 F3): the measured nodes DISAGREE, and an
    91	 * engine that picks one silently moves findings. Three measured orders:
    92	 *
    93	 * - `missingThenUnknown`             — the root keyset
    94	 * - `unknownThenPerKey`              — ref, round, activation, the
    95	 *                                      runtimeContext spec, every gate
    96	 *                                      config (the DEFAULT)
    97	 * - `unknownThenMissingThenValues`   — the step keyset
    98	 */
    99	export type LaneOrder =
   100	  | "missingThenUnknown"
   101	  | "unknownThenPerKey"
   102	  | "unknownThenMissingThenValues";
   103	
   104	/** Every node carries its declaration TAG (the contract's pointer target,
   105	 * D4) and the ratified rows it realizes (the tag-closure check's other
   106	 * half). `channel` marks a channel-SCOPED node: `file` for a
   107	 * SOURCE-BEARING attribute set (the engine runs it where a source exists
   108	 * and skips it where none does), `direct` for a key that exists only on
   109	 * the direct-construction channel because it belongs to the ADMITTED form
   110	 * rather than the authored one. This is D1's structural channel symmetry
   111	 * — the retired hand-partitioned "realization split". */
   112	interface NodeBase {
   113	  readonly tag: string;
   114	  readonly rows: readonly string[];
   115	  readonly channel?: "both" | "file" | "direct";
   116	  /** vocabulary #9 — materialized ONCE at admission. Plain `default:` is
   117	   * declarable and stays schema-side (ADR-019 D3); DERIVATION is the
   118	   * normalizer's. */
   119	  readonly default?: unknown;
   120	  /** vocabulary #13 — this node's failure suppresses its dependents. */
   121	  readonly gating?: boolean;
   122	  /**
   123	   * vocabulary #2 — the keyset membership obligation, declared on the
   124	   * FIELD rather than on its container, because the measured lanes
   125	   * disagree on both grains: the template's fixed maps report a missing
   126	   * key at the CONTAINER with one shared wording, every delegated config
   127	   * schema reports it at the KEY with its own wording, and one node (a
   128	   * gate binding's `uses`) folds absence into its own type lane.
   129	   */
   130	  readonly presence?: {
   131	    readonly required: true;
   132	    readonly at?: "container" | "self";
   133	    readonly message?: MessageTemplate;
   134	    readonly code?: string;
   135	    /** Absence is reported by this node's OWN type lane, not by a
   136	     * separate missing lane (a gate binding's `uses`). */
   137	    readonly foldedIntoTypeLane?: true;
   138	  };
   139	}
   140	
   141	/** vocabulary #1 `map.fixed` — a closed keyset. The legal keyset IS
   142	 * `Object.keys(fields)`; each field declares its own `presence`. */
   143	export interface MapFixedDecl extends NodeBase {
   144	  readonly kind: "map.fixed";
   145	  readonly containerMessage: MessageTemplate;
   146	  /** The default missing-key wording for fields whose `presence` states
   147	   * none. */
   148	  readonly missingMessage?: MessageTemplate;
   149	  readonly unknownMessage: MessageTemplate;
   150	  readonly laneOrder?: LaneOrder;
   151	  readonly fields: Readonly<Record<string, NodeDecl>>;
   152	  /** vocabulary #10 — fail-loud key removal with migration text. */
   153	  readonly removedKeys?: Readonly<Record<string, MessageTemplate>>;
   154	}
   155	
   156	/** vocabulary #1 `map.open` — an open key class. */
   157	export interface MapOpenDecl extends NodeBase {
   158	  readonly kind: "map.open";
   159	  readonly containerMessage: MessageTemplate;
   160	  /** vocabulary #3 on a map. `gating` is OPT-IN and the template surface
   161	   * does not use it: ch8-C21's suppression binds MISSING or WRONG-KIND
   162	   * containers only, so an empty map is a valid map of its kind and every
   163	   * rule selecting over its (empty) key set still runs. The attribute
   164	   * exists for a surface that ratifies the opposite; declaring it is what
   165	   * makes an empty container gating, never the engine's own choice. */
   166	  readonly nonempty?: { readonly message: MessageTemplate; readonly gating?: boolean };
   167	  /** vocabulary #5 — the open-map key class and the finding's path grain. */
   168	  readonly keyClass?: ValueClassRefDecl;
   169	  readonly keyLaneAt: "container" | "segment";
   170	  /** The gates subtree's file-channel key-STRINGNESS scan (ch11 walk /
   171	   * GP2): a DEEP scan reporting at the nearest addressable path. */
   172	  readonly deepKeyStringness?: {
   173	    readonly message: MessageTemplate;
   174	    readonly channel: "file";
   175	  };
   176	  readonly keysSubsetOf?: MembershipRule;
   177	  readonly entry: NodeDecl;
   178	}
   179	
   180	/** vocabulary #1 `list`. */
   181	export interface ListDecl extends NodeBase {
   182	  readonly kind: "list";
   183	  readonly containerMessage: MessageTemplate;
   184	  /** See `MapOpenDecl.nonempty`: `gating` is opt-in and unused on this
   185	   * surface. */
   186	  readonly nonempty?: { readonly message: MessageTemplate; readonly gating?: boolean };
   187	  readonly member: NodeDecl;
   188	  /** The member lane's path grain — `container` reports every member at
   189	   * the list's own path (ch8-C17's measured grain). */
   190	  readonly memberLaneAt: "container" | "index";
   191	  readonly unique?: UniqueRule;
   192	  readonly disjointFrom?: MembershipRule;
   193	  readonly memberOf?: MembershipRule;
   194	}
   195	
   196	/** vocabulary #1 `string` (+ #3 `nonempty`, #4 `grammar`). */
   197	export interface StringDecl extends NodeBase {
   198	  readonly kind: "string";
   199	  /** The non-string lane's message. When a node states only `grammar`,
   200	   * the grammar message covers both lanes (ch8-C8's measured form). */
   201	  readonly typeMessage?: MessageTemplate;
   202	  readonly nonempty?: { readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   203	  readonly grammar?: { readonly re: string; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   204	  readonly memberOf?: MembershipRule;
   205	}
   206	
   207	/** vocabulary #6/#7 — the raw-source ladder and the value-side belt. */
   208	export interface IntegerDecl extends NodeBase {
   209	  readonly kind: "integer";
   210	  /** The alias-free / anchor-free / tag-free / `^[1-9][0-9]*$` ladder over
   211	   * the SOURCE node. File-scoped by construction: no operand exists on the
   212	   * direct-construction channel. */
   213	  readonly sourceForm?: "plainDecimalInteger";
   214	  readonly resolvedForm?: {
   215	    readonly safeInteger: true;
   216	    readonly min: number;
   217	    readonly message: MessageTemplate;
   218	  };
   219	}
   220	
   221	/** vocabulary #8 — an allowlist. `store` is the authored↔stored token map
   222	 * (ch12-C1). */
   223	export interface EnumDecl extends NodeBase {
   224	  readonly kind: "enum";
   225	  /** A member may be channel-SCOPED: the same `channel` attribute the node
   226	   * grain carries, applied at MEMBER grain. It is a widening, not a new
   227	   * construct — the measured token domain genuinely differs by channel
   228	   * (ch12-C1: the FILE face is authored camelCase, the direct face is the
   229	   * stored token the domain type declares). */
   230	  readonly members: readonly {
   231	    readonly value: unknown;
   232	    readonly store?: string;
   233	    readonly channel?: "both" | "file" | "direct";
   234	  }[];
   235	  readonly message: MessageTemplate;
   236	  readonly code?: string;
   237	}
   238	
   239	/** vocabulary #1 `union` (+ #10 `removed` at VALUE grain). */
   240	export interface UnionDecl extends NodeBase {
   241	  readonly kind: "union";
   242	  readonly literals?: readonly string[];
   243	  readonly mapCase?: NodeDecl;
   244	  readonly removedValues?: Readonly<Record<string, MessageTemplate>>;
   245	  readonly message: MessageTemplate;
   246	}
   247	
   248	/** vocabulary #19 — uninterpreted pass-through. */
   249	export interface RawDecl extends NodeBase {
   250	  readonly kind: "raw";
   251	  /** A raw node may still assert a container kind (ch12-C3's `config`). */
   252	  readonly containerMessage?: MessageTemplate;
   253	}
   254	
   255	/** `map.plain + canonicalJsonSafe` — the agent-config value class
   256	 * (ch12-C7). A PLAIN string-keyed map (never a class instance, never a
   257	 * JS Map); a non-plain container SUPPRESSES the canonical lane. */
   258	export interface MapPlainDecl extends NodeBase {
   259	  readonly kind: "map.plain";
   260	  readonly containerMessage: MessageTemplate;
   261	  readonly canonicalJsonSafe: { readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   262	}
   263	
   264	/** vocabulary #15 — a reusable named value class, with the citing site's
   265	 * `{label}`. */
   266	export interface ValueClassRefDecl extends NodeBase {
   267	  readonly kind: "valueClass";
   268	  readonly valueClass: string;
   269	  readonly label?: string;
   270	}
   271	
   272	/** vocabulary #16 — hand-off to an injected registration's own
   273	 * declaration. */
   274	export interface DelegateDecl extends NodeBase {
   275	  readonly kind: "delegate";
   276	  readonly registry: "gateCatalog";
   277	  /** The sibling field whose value names the registration. */
   278	  readonly by: string;
   279	  /** The delegation contract's own belt: a registration that reports
   280	   * failure with ZERO findings must still block admission, or the binding
   281	   * would be admitted with no effective config. A property of the
   282	   * `delegate` construct, not of any one row (audit flag I3). */
   283	  readonly beltMessage: MessageTemplate;
   284	  /** vocabulary #13 — the resolution lane this hand-off presupposes. */
   285	  readonly dependsOn?: readonly string[];
   286	}
   287	
   288	export type NodeDecl =
   289	  | MapFixedDecl
   290	  | MapOpenDecl
   291	  | MapPlainDecl
   292	  | ListDecl
   293	  | StringDecl
   294	  | IntegerDecl
   295	  | EnumDecl
   296	  | UnionDecl
   297	  | RawDecl
   298	  | ValueClassRefDecl
   299	  | DelegateDecl;
   300	
   301	/**
   302	 * vocabulary #12's `equals` relation — a two-direction set equality with
   303	 * its own message per direction and its own path grain per direction
   304	 * (ch8-C16's measured form: the used-but-undeclared finding reports at
   305	 * the container, the declared-but-unused finding at the entry).
   306	 */
   307	export interface EqualsRuleDecl {
   308	  readonly tag: string;
   309	  readonly rows: readonly string[];
   310	  readonly relation: "equals";
   311	  readonly left: Selector;
   312	  readonly right: Selector;
   313	  readonly missingFromLeft: { readonly at: DeclPath; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   314	  readonly missingFromRight: { readonly at: DeclPath; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   315	  readonly dependsOn?: readonly string[];
   316	}
   317	
   318	/**
   319	 * D3 — the NORMALIZER's declared hooks. A declaration says what is legal;
   320	 * it does not compute a value, so derivation is named here and realized in
   321	 * `normalizer.ts`, never in the validator. Each hook is a named capability
   322	 * with its ratified rows; adding one is D7 format growth, not an edit.
   323	 */
   324	export type NormalizerHookDecl =
   325	  | {
   326	      readonly tag: string;
   327	      readonly rows: readonly string[];
   328	      readonly hook: "expandAdvancesRound";
   329	      /** The open map of entries the flag map is expanded per. */
   330	      readonly over: DeclPath;
   331	      /** The entry's edge map, relative to the entry. */
   332	      readonly edges: string;
   333	      /** The declared advancing set. */
   334	      readonly advanceSet: DeclPath;
   335	      /** The produced sibling field. */
   336	      readonly into: string;
   337	    }
   338	  | {
   339	      readonly tag: string;
   340	      readonly rows: readonly string[];
   341	      readonly hook: "materializeEffectiveConfigs";
   342	      /** The open map of pipelines whose members carry a delegated config. */
   343	      readonly over: DeclPath;
   344	      /** The binding fields carried into the admitted form verbatim. */
   345	      readonly carry: readonly string[];
   346	      /** The produced field the effective config lands in. */
   347	      readonly into: string;
   348	    };
   349	
   350	/**
   351	 * The SUBSTRATE block (audit §2.3): how bytes become a value graph. Its
   352	 * obligations are declared here as data; the yaml library realizes them,
   353	 * which is why their WORDING is residual R6 (library-owned) rather than
   354	 * engine parity.
   355	 */
   356	export interface SubstrateDecl {
   357	  /** ch8-C6: the read stage's decode wording. */
   358	  readonly read: { readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   359	  readonly parse: {
   360	    /** ch8-C34: the only accepted `%YAML` directive version, and the
   361	     * finding a different one produces (`{key}` is the authored one). */
   362	    readonly directive: { readonly only: string; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   363	    /** ch8-C4: the duplicate-key wording. */
   364	    readonly duplicateKeyMessage: MessageTemplate;
   365	  };
   366	  /** ch8-C5: the resolved graph must be acyclic, and the finding it
   367	   * yields at the cycle's path. */
   368	  readonly resolve: { readonly graph: { readonly message: MessageTemplate } };
   369	  /** ch8-C23: the CLOSED issue-code namespace. Every `code` any node in
   370	   * the surface declares must be a member — asserted, not assumed. */
   371	  readonly codes: readonly string[];
   372	  /** ch8-C22/C36: the every-stage belt's own finding. */
   373	  readonly internalFailure: { readonly path: string; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   374	}
   375	
   376	/** One declared surface: substrate + node tree + the named residual's
   377	 * declarable halves. */
   378	export interface SurfaceDecl {
   379	  readonly substrate: SubstrateDecl;
   380	  readonly root: NodeDecl;
   381	  readonly valueClasses: Readonly<Record<string, NodeDecl>>;
   382	  readonly crossRules: readonly EqualsRuleDecl[];
   383	  readonly normalizers: readonly NormalizerHookDecl[];
   384	}

     1	/**
     2	 * ADR-019 (accepted 2026-08-05): the declaration VOCABULARY, as types.
     3	 *
     4	 * This module is TYPES ONLY — it defines the shape a surface declaration
     5	 * may take, and nothing else. The declaration itself is data
     6	 * (`templateFormat.ts`, D4's canonical home); the machines that consume
     7	 * it are `engine.ts` (the validator) and `normalizer.ts` (D3's second,
     8	 * separately-named capability).
     9	 *
    10	 * Every construct below is one of ADR-019 §2.2's admitted vocabulary
    11	 * entries, each of which passed the audit's ≥2-independent-ratified-rows
    12	 * test (`v3/implementation/schema-expressiveness-audit.md` §2.2, standing
    13	 * sha256 `bfed8210…`). Adding a construct is a D7 amendment of the ADR,
    14	 * never a silent edit here.
    15	 */

    17	/**
    18	 * A message template: literal text with `{placeholder}` slots the engine
    19	 * interpolates (vocabulary #18 — `message:`, the PARITY carrier; ADR-019
    20	 * D5 pre-names message wording as a delta class, and a literal template
    21	 * is the choice that keeps the measured wording byte-identical).
    22	 *
    23	 * The closed placeholder set, all supplied by the engine from the node's
    24	 * own evaluation context — never rule-specific:
    25	 *
    26	 * - `{path}`      the finding's own path
    27	 * - `{key}`       the offending key, raw
    28	 * - `{keyJson}`   the offending key, JSON-quoted
    29	 * - `{value}`     the offending value, DESCRIBED (`a map`/`a list`/literal)
    30	 * - `{valueRaw}`  the offending value, rendered bare (no quoting) — the
    31	 *                 form the measured messages that embed a value inside
    32	 *                 their own quotes need
    33	 * - `{valueJson}` the offending value, JSON-quoted
    34	 * - `{source}`    the offending value's raw source slice, JSON-quoted
    35	 * - `{grammar}`   the grammar's regular-expression source
    36	 * - `{members}`   the enum/allowlist members, comma-joined
    37	 * - `{keys}`      the node's legal keyset, comma-joined
    38	 * - `{label}`     the referencing site's label for a value class
    39	 * - `{ownerKey}`  the key of the nearest enclosing OPEN-map entry
    40	 */
    41	export type MessageTemplate = string;
    42	
    43	/**
    44	 * A path into the value graph, in the declaration's own address form:
    45	 * `$` root, dotted segments, `*` for "every entry of an open map", and a
    46	 * leading `..` for one level up from the citing node.
    47	 */
    48	export type DeclPath = string;
    49	
    50	/** vocabulary #12 — the selector roots. `injected` is D8's ADMITTED
    51	 * widening: a selector root that is an injected SET rather than a
    52	 * document node (a set is a set; the relation and finding form are
    53	 * unchanged). */
    54	export type Selector =
    55	  | { readonly keysOf: DeclPath }
    56	  | { readonly valuesOf: DeclPath }
    57	  | { readonly collect: DeclPath }
    58	  | { readonly union: readonly Selector[] }
    59	  | { readonly injected: "gateCatalog" };
    60	
    61	/**
    62	 * vocabulary #12 — a membership relation between the citing node's value
    63	 * (or its keys) and a selector's set.
    64	 *
    65	 * `at` is the finding's PATH GRAIN and exists because the measured lanes
    66	 * DISAGREE (audit §5 F3): the catalog-resolution lane reports at the
    67	 * BINDING, not at `…uses`. `code` is vocabulary #17.
    68	 */
    69	export interface MembershipRule {
    70	  readonly relation: "memberOf" | "keysSubsetOf" | "disjointFrom";
    71	  readonly target: Selector;
    72	  /** `self` = the citing node's own path (the default); `container` = its
    73	   * parent's path. */
    74	  readonly at?: "self" | "container";
    75	  readonly code?: string;
    76	  readonly message: MessageTemplate;
    77	  /** vocabulary #13 — suppression beyond the implicit container rule. */
    78	  readonly dependsOn?: readonly string[];
    79	}
    80	
    81	/** vocabulary #11 — the duplicate lane and its path grain. */
    82	export interface UniqueRule {
    83	  readonly grain: "perOccurrence";
    84	  readonly at: "index" | "container";
    85	  readonly message: MessageTemplate;
    86	}
    87	
    88	/**
    89	 * The keyset lane ORDER. It is declared, not hard-coded, for the same
    90	 * reason `at:` is (audit §5 F3): the measured nodes DISAGREE, and an
    91	 * engine that picks one silently moves findings. Three measured orders:
    92	 *
    93	 * - `missingThenUnknown`             — the root keyset
    94	 * - `unknownThenPerKey`              — ref, round, activation, the
    95	 *                                      runtimeContext spec, every gate
    96	 *                                      config (the DEFAULT)
    97	 * - `unknownThenMissingThenValues`   — the step keyset
    98	 */
    99	export type LaneOrder =
   100	  | "missingThenUnknown"
   101	  | "unknownThenPerKey"
   102	  | "unknownThenMissingThenValues";
   103	
   104	/** Every node carries its declaration TAG (the contract's pointer target,
   105	 * D4) and the ratified rows it realizes (the tag-closure check's other
   106	 * half). `channel` marks a channel-SCOPED node: `file` for a
   107	 * SOURCE-BEARING attribute set (the engine runs it where a source exists
   108	 * and skips it where none does), `direct` for a key that exists only on
   109	 * the direct-construction channel because it belongs to the ADMITTED form
   110	 * rather than the authored one. This is D1's structural channel symmetry
   111	 * — the retired hand-partitioned "realization split". */
   112	interface NodeBase {
   113	  readonly tag: string;
   114	  readonly rows: readonly string[];
   115	  readonly channel?: "both" | "file" | "direct";
   116	  /** vocabulary #9 — materialized ONCE at admission. Plain `default:` is
   117	   * declarable and stays schema-side (ADR-019 D3); DERIVATION is the
   118	   * normalizer's. */
   119	  readonly default?: unknown;
   120	  /** vocabulary #13 — this node's failure suppresses its dependents. */
   121	  readonly gating?: boolean;
   122	  /**
   123	   * vocabulary #2 — the keyset membership obligation, declared on the
   124	   * FIELD rather than on its container, because the measured lanes
   125	   * disagree on both grains: the template's fixed maps report a missing
   126	   * key at the CONTAINER with one shared wording, every delegated config
   127	   * schema reports it at the KEY with its own wording, and one node (a
   128	   * gate binding's `uses`) folds absence into its own type lane.
   129	   */
   130	  readonly presence?: {
   131	    readonly required: true;
   132	    readonly at?: "container" | "self";
   133	    readonly message?: MessageTemplate;
   134	    readonly code?: string;
   135	    /** Absence is reported by this node's OWN type lane, not by a
   136	     * separate missing lane (a gate binding's `uses`). */
   137	    readonly foldedIntoTypeLane?: true;
   138	  };
   139	}

   141	/** vocabulary #1 `map.fixed` — a closed keyset. The legal keyset IS
   142	 * `Object.keys(fields)`; each field declares its own `presence`. */
   143	export interface MapFixedDecl extends NodeBase {
   144	  readonly kind: "map.fixed";
   145	  readonly containerMessage: MessageTemplate;
   146	  /** The default missing-key wording for fields whose `presence` states
   147	   * none. */
   148	  readonly missingMessage?: MessageTemplate;
   149	  readonly unknownMessage: MessageTemplate;
   150	  readonly laneOrder?: LaneOrder;
   151	  readonly fields: Readonly<Record<string, NodeDecl>>;
   152	  /** vocabulary #10 — fail-loud key removal with migration text. */
   153	  readonly removedKeys?: Readonly<Record<string, MessageTemplate>>;
   154	}

   172	  readonly deepKeyStringness?: {

   174	    readonly channel: "file";

   301	/**
   302	 * vocabulary #12's `equals` relation — a two-direction set equality with
   303	 * its own message per direction and its own path grain per direction
   304	 * (ch8-C16's measured form: the used-but-undeclared finding reports at
   305	 * the container, the declared-but-unused finding at the entry).
   306	 */
   307	export interface EqualsRuleDecl {
   308	  readonly tag: string;
   309	  readonly rows: readonly string[];

   315	  readonly dependsOn?: readonly string[];

   318	/**
   319	 * D3 — the NORMALIZER's declared hooks. A declaration says what is legal;
   320	 * it does not compute a value, so derivation is named here and realized in
   321	 * `normalizer.ts`, never in the validator. Each hook is a named capability
   322	 * with its ratified rows; adding one is D7 format growth, not an edit.
   323	 */
   324	export type NormalizerHookDecl =
   325	  | {
   326	      readonly tag: string;
   327	      readonly rows: readonly string[];
   328	      readonly hook: "expandAdvancesRound";
   329	      /** The open map of entries the flag map is expanded per. */
   330	      readonly over: DeclPath;
   331	      /** The entry's edge map, relative to the entry. */
   332	      readonly edges: string;
   333	      /** The declared advancing set. */
   334	      readonly advanceSet: DeclPath;
   335	      /** The produced sibling field. */
   336	      readonly into: string;
   337	    }
   338	  | {
   339	      readonly tag: string;
   340	      readonly rows: readonly string[];
   341	      readonly hook: "materializeEffectiveConfigs";
   342	      /** The open map of pipelines whose members carry a delegated config. */
   343	      readonly over: DeclPath;
   344	      /** The binding fields carried into the admitted form verbatim. */
   345	      readonly carry: readonly string[];
   346	      /** The produced field the effective config lands in. */
   347	      readonly into: string;
   348	    };

   350	/**
   351	 * The SUBSTRATE block (audit §2.3): how bytes become a value graph. Its
   352	 * obligations are declared here as data; the yaml library realizes them,
   353	 * which is why their WORDING is residual R6 (library-owned) rather than
   354	 * engine parity.
   355	 */
   356	export interface SubstrateDecl {
   357	  /** ch8-C6: the read stage's decode wording. */
   358	  readonly read: { readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   359	  readonly parse: {
   360	    /** ch8-C34: the only accepted `%YAML` directive version, and the
   361	     * finding a different one produces (`{key}` is the authored one). */
   362	    readonly directive: { readonly only: string; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   363	    /** ch8-C4: the duplicate-key wording. */
   364	    readonly duplicateKeyMessage: MessageTemplate;
   365	  };
   366	  /** ch8-C5: the resolved graph must be acyclic, and the finding it
   367	   * yields at the cycle's path. */
   368	  readonly resolve: { readonly graph: { readonly message: MessageTemplate } };
   369	  /** ch8-C23: the CLOSED issue-code namespace. Every `code` any node in
   370	   * the surface declares must be a member — asserted, not assumed. */
   371	  readonly codes: readonly string[];
   372	  /** ch8-C22/C36: the every-stage belt's own finding. */
   373	  readonly internalFailure: { readonly path: string; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   374	}

   376	/** One declared surface: substrate + node tree + the named residual's
   377	 * declarable halves. */
   378	export interface SurfaceDecl {
   379	  readonly substrate: SubstrateDecl;
   380	  readonly root: NodeDecl;
   381	  readonly valueClasses: Readonly<Record<string, NodeDecl>>;
   382	  readonly crossRules: readonly EqualsRuleDecl[];
   383	  readonly normalizers: readonly NormalizerHookDecl[];
   384	}
     1	import type { NodeDecl, SurfaceDecl } from "./vocabulary.js";
     2	
     3	/**
     4	 * ADR-019 D4: THE CANONICAL HOME — the template surface's declaration, as
     5	 * DATA. This module contains no executable logic: every export is a frozen
     6	 * declaration object consumed by `engine.ts` (the validator) and
     7	 * `normalizer.ts` (the derivation capability, D3).
     8	 *
     9	 * The two-authority rule (D4): the CONTRACT states decisions and cites the
    10	 * `tag` values below; it restates no attribute. A structural rule visible
    11	 * in both places is a defect, and the tag-closure check
    12	 * (`collectTags` in `engine.ts`) is its tripwire.
    13	 *
    14	 * Every node below realizes the re-expression column of
    15	 * `v3/implementation/schema-expressiveness-audit.md` (standing sha256
    16	 * `bfed8210…`) and carries the ratified rows it realizes in `rows`. The
    17	 * audited residual stays PROSE/CODE and is NOT declared here: R1 (the
    18	 * value-shaped reference belt), R2 (unreferenced hygiene), R3 (the
    19	 * existential cross-rule over resolved registrations), R4 (derivation —
    20	 * the normalizer's), R5 (the cross-artifact store check), R6 (substrate
    21	 * wording), R7 (see `d-gate-config`'s note).
    22	 */
    23	
    24	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    25	// Value classes (vocabulary #15).
    26	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    27	
    28	/** ch8-C10: ONE grammar for every id class — nonempty, no whitespace, no
    29	 * dot. The citing site supplies `{label}`. */
    30	const idClass: NodeDecl = {
    31	  kind: "string",
    32	  tag: "vc-id-class",
    33	  rows: ["ch8-C10"],
    34	  typeMessage: "{label} must be a nonempty string, got {value}",

    43	 * values are canonical-JSON-safe. A non-plain container suppresses the
    44	 * canonical lane (dependent-lane suppression). */

    46	  kind: "map.plain",

    55	/**
    56	 * NOT DECLARED HERE, and the reason is a REPO GUARD, not taste: the three
    57	 * delegated gate-config schemas (`declarative.threshold`,
    58	 * `pairflow.previous_reviewer_verdict`, `external.process`) live behind
    59	 * ch11-C5's `delegate: registry(uses)` hand-off, and ch11-P2a G1 —
    60	 * lint-enforced — permits `gates/` to VALUE-import `domain/` and `ports/`
    61	 * only. A registration cannot consume an engine that lives under
    62	 * `definition/`, and moving the engine into `domain/` or `ports/` to make
    63	 * it reachable would be a worse answer than leaving the hand-off alone.
    64	 *
    65	 * Under ADR-019 D2 (the TEMPLATE surface, now; nothing else is built) and
    66	 * D6 (migrating a surface is a deliberate per-surface act, never a "while
    67	 * we are here"), the three schemas stay as built. The audit's paper
    68	 * declarations `[d-gc-threshold]`, `[d-gc-verdict]` and `[d-gc-process]`
    69	 * remain available to the act that migrates them.
    70	 */

   479	export const templateFormat: SurfaceDecl = Object.freeze({
   480	  // The SUBSTRATE block carries only what is consumed or asserted. The
   481	  // rules it used to state about the yaml library's own behaviour — the
   482	  // core schema, one document per file, warning promotion, alias
   483	  // resolution — are real ratified rules (ch8-C1/C2/C3/C5) but they are
   484	  // the LIBRARY's behaviour, not a knob anything here reads; stating them
   485	  // as declaration made them read as authority they were not (arm round
   486	  // 1, F5). Their test carriers are `load.test.ts`'s substrate lanes.
   487	  substrate: {
   488	    read: { message: "invalid UTF-8 byte sequence (templates are strict UTF-8)" },
   489	    parse: {
   490	      directive: { only: "1.2", message: "%YAML {key} directive: only YAML 1.2 is supported" },
   491	      duplicateKeyMessage: "Map keys must be unique",
   492	    },
   493	    resolve: {
   494	      graph: { message: "cyclic value structure: the resolved template graph must be acyclic" },
   495	    },
   496	    // ch8-C23: the CLOSED issue-code namespace, disjoint from registry
   497	    // names. `unresolved_context_block_ref` is ratified-but-unbuilt.
   498	    codes: [
   499	      "gate_evaluator_unavailable",
   500	      "runtime_context_required_for_process_gate",
   501	      "invalid_process_gate_config",
   502	      "gate_config_not_supported",
   503	      "unresolved_context_block_ref",
   504	    ],
   505	    internalFailure: { path: "$", message: "internal validator failure: {valueRaw}" },
   506	  },
   507	  root: rootNode,
   508	  valueClasses: { idClass, agentConfigValue },
   509	  crossRules: [
   510	    {
   511	      tag: "d-roleset",
   512	      rows: ["ch8-C16"],
   513	      relation: "equals",
   514	      left: { keysOf: "$.roles" },
   515	      right: { collect: "$.steps.*.role" },
   516	      missingFromLeft: {
   517	        at: "roles",
   518	        message: "role {valueJson} is used by steps but not declared",
   519	      },
   520	      missingFromRight: {
   521	        at: "roles.{valueRaw}",
   522	        message: "role {valueJson} is declared but not used by any step",
   523	      },
   524	    },
   525	  ],
   526	  normalizers: [
   527	    {
   528	      tag: "n-advances-round",
   529	      rows: ["ch11-C39", "ch11-C38"],
   530	      hook: "expandAdvancesRound",
   531	      over: "$.steps",
   532	      edges: "transitions",
   533	      advanceSet: "$.round.advanceOnArrivalAt",
   534	      into: "advancesRound",
   535	    },
   536	    {
   537	      tag: "n-effective-config",
   538	      rows: ["ch11-C20", "ch11-C5"],
   539	      hook: "materializeEffectiveConfigs",
   540	      over: "$.steps.*.gates",
   541	      carry: ["uses"],
   542	      into: "config",
   543	    },
   544	  ],
   545	} satisfies SurfaceDecl);

codex
### F1 — A „strukturálisan lehetetlen restatement” valójában csak egy kijelölt normatív hely

CLASS: over-promise  
LENS:  1a  
WHAT: Az egy engine megszünteti a két belépési csatorna külön validator-implementációját, de nem teszi strukturálisan lehetetlenné ugyanannak a szabálynak a második normatív megfogalmazását; ezt csak a D4 szerkesztési szabálya tiltja.  
WHERE: `v3/adr/ADR-019-declared-schema-for-structural-definition-rules.md:19`, `:111-138`, `:264-269`; `v3/src/definition/schema/templateFormat.ts:9-12`

### F2 — A deklaráció csak sekélyen fagyasztott

CLASS: over-promise  
LENS:  1b  
WHAT: A D4 „frozen declaration object” állítása csak a `templateFormat` legkülső objektumára igaz; a teljes szabályfa, a külön definiált node-ok, tömbök és üzenet-/szabályobjektumok futásidőben módosíthatók.  
WHERE: `v3/adr/ADR-019-declared-schema-for-structural-definition-rules.md:113-119`; `v3/src/definition/schema/templateFormat.ts:479-545`  
EVIDENCE:
  `node -e 'const x=Object.freeze({nested:{value:1}}); x.nested.value=2; console.log(JSON.stringify({rootFrozen:Object.isFrozen(x),nestedFrozen:Object.isFrozen(x.nested),value:x.nested.value}))'`
  kimenete: `{"rootFrozen":true,"nestedFrozen":false,"value":2}`. A repóban a deklarációt egyetlen `Object.freeze({...})` fogja körbe; nincs rekurzív fagyasztás, miközben a projekt más tesztjei külön `deepFreeze` segédet használnak.  
WHY IT MATTERS: Egy importáló modul vagy teszt például a `templateFormat.root.fields.start.memberOf.target` értékét folyamaton belül átírhatja úgy, hogy az ezt követő összes admission már más szabályt hajt végre; a byte-lock és a source diff ezt nem látja, ezért a „frozen authority” futásidejű garanciája nem áll fenn.
